n^{\prime}<n
such that

n^{\prime}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v^{\prime})
.
We use part
2
of this result.
∎

Proposition 7.3.3
.

Suppose
\varsigma
is a valuation such that
\vDash_{\varsigma}\text{TyPax}
.
Suppose
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle\geq 0}
and
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathord{\text{Value}}
.
Then

n\vDash_{\varsigma}({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v^{\prime}))\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}v{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v^{\prime}.

Proof.

We use strong modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
2
)).
Suppose

n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v^{\prime}))
.
Using
{\mathsf{(PaxDecide{\boldsymbol{\neg}}L?)}}
if needed (as we did in the proof of Theorem
7.2.3
), and then
{\mathsf{(PaxDecideL?)}}
twice, we have

n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v^{\prime}))
and thus by Proposition
4.4.3
(
4
) and Lemma
4.2.1

also
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v^{\prime}))
.
We can now use Lemma
7.3.2
(
2
).
∎

Remark 7.3.4
.

Proposition
7.3.3
proves part of the agreement property; that no two correct participants decide differently
at a given time
.
To prove the result across different times will require a bit more work, culminating with Theorem
7.3.6
.

7.3.2
Some fine structure of the rules

We continue a discussion from Remark
6.5.1
(
2
) and also Corollary
6.6.7
from Section
6.6.3
.

Note that we use
{\mathsf{(LdrUniq)}}
from Figure
11
just once: in the proof of Lemma
7.3.2
(
1
), via Corollary
6.6.7
(
3
).

Examining how we use it, we see that for our specific purposes it would suffice to assume a more parsimonious axiom (both in the sense that it is simpler, and in the sense that it permits more models) as follows:

\hskip-60.00009pt{\mathsf{(LdrExtWrite)}}\qquad\qquad{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.(\mathsf{leader}\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}(\mathsf{leader}\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{write}(v))\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}\mathsf{write}(v)).

Recall from Subsection
6.6.3
that axiom
{\mathsf{(LdrUniq)}}
asserts that there is a unique leader at each stage (actually, at most one;
{\mathsf{(LdrExist)}}
does the rest).
In contrast,
{\mathsf{(LdrExtWrite)}}
above asserts more weakly that there is a unique leader at each stage, up to extensional equivalence
for just writing values
.
In the proofs, this turns out to be all we need.

In fact, we can simplify this just to

\hskip-60.00009pt{\mathsf{(SPaxWrite01)}}\qquad\qquad{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists_{01}$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}.

In full, this is
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists_{01}$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}(v)
, and in words it expresses:

‘at each stage, at most one value is written’.

In the presence of
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite?)}}
and
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite01)}}
,
{\mathsf{(SPaxWrite01)}}
is implied by
{\mathsf{(LdrExtWrite)}}
— but it does not imply
{\mathsf{(LdrExtWrite)}}
, because it permits the possibility that one leader participant writes a value and another leader participant crashes.
The reader can check that
{\mathsf{(SPaxWrite01)}}
is sufficient to complete the proof of Lemma
7.3.2
(
1
).

Note also that
{\mathsf{(SPaxWrite01)}}
implies
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite01)}}
, so that — for the proofs we care about —
{\mathsf{(SPaxWrite01)}}
can replace both
{\mathsf{(LdrUniq)}}
and
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite01)}}
.
We will do exactly this, later: the job of
\Theta_{\text{TyPax}}
is to be ‘declarative Paxos’, that is, its purpose is to be declarative
and
to be recognisably Paxos.
Later on in Figure
12
we will define a
simpler
declarative Paxos (whence the name ‘SPaxWrite01’).
This simplified theory is derived by looking at the proofs here, and seeing what we actually require.

The journey from
{\mathsf{(LdrExist)}}
+
{\mathsf{(LdrUniq)}}
to
{\mathsf{(SPaxWrite01)}}
is the usual mathematical journey from something relatively concrete, to an abstraction of that thing.
Of course
{\mathsf{(LdrExist)}}
+
{\mathsf{(LdrUniq)}}
themselves are axiomatic abstractions of the programmatic fact that in a Paxos implementation, there
is
precisely one leader.
How much detail we choose to retain, and how much we choose to dispense with, is a matter of taste, judgement, and the intended use-case.
Logic gives us freedom to make these choices as best suits our needs.

7.3.3
Agreement at possibly different times

Proposition 7.3.5
.

Suppose
\varsigma
is a valuation such that
\vDash_{\varsigma}\text{TyPax}
, and suppose
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathord{\text{Value}}
.
Then:

1.

\vDash_{\varsigma}{\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v^{\prime})\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}v^{\prime}{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v
.

2.

\vDash_{\varsigma}{\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}(v^{\prime})\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}v^{\prime}{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v
.

Proof.

Suppose
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle\geq 0}
.
We will reason using strong modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
2
)), so suppose

n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v).

(1)

We will prove by induction on
n^{\prime}>n
that for every
v^{\prime}\in\mathord{\text{Value}}
,

\begin{array}[]{r@{\ }l}n^{\prime}\vDash_{\varsigma}&\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt(({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept})\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v^{\prime}\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}v^{\prime}{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v)\quad\text{and}\\
n^{\prime}\vDash_{\varsigma}&\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}(v^{\prime})\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}v^{\prime}{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v)\end{array}

Clearly, by strong modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
2
)) the
\mathsf{write}
part of the inductive hypothesis is enough to prove part
2
of this result.
For the
\mathsf{accept}
part of the inductive hypothesis, we see that this implies part
1
of this result either by a routine argument using
{\mathsf{(PaxAccept?)}}
and part
2
of this result — or using Corollary
4.9.6
(
1
) (for
a.\phi=a.{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(a)
in that result).

We consider cases:

•

The base case for
\mathsf{accept}
, where
n^{\prime}=n+1
.

Suppose
n^{\prime}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v^{\prime})
.

We assumed in equation (
1
) that
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)
, so by Proposition
4.4.3
(
4
) also
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v)
, and by Corollary
4.9.6
(
1
)
n^{\prime}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt(({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept})\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v)
.
By Lemma
7.3.2
(
3
)
v^{\prime}=v
.

•

The inductive step for
\mathsf{accept}
, where
n^{\prime}>n+1
.

Suppose
n^{\prime}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v^{\prime})
.
By Corollary
4.9.6
(
2
) either
n^{\prime}{\shortminus}1\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v^{\prime})
or
n^{\prime}{\shortminus}1\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v^{\prime})
.

In the former case, using
{\mathsf{(PaxAccept?)}}

n^{\prime}{\shortminus}1\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}(v^{\prime})
, and by the
\mathsf{write}
part of the inductive hypothesis,
v^{\prime}=v
.
In the latter case, we just use the
\mathsf{accept}
part of the inductive hypothesis.

•

The case of
\mathsf{write}
.

Suppose
p\in\mathord{\text{Point}}
and
n^{\prime},p\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{write}(v^{\prime})
.
Using
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite?)}}
, there exists an
O\in\mathord{\text{Open}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\scalebox{0.6}{\pdfliteral${\neq}\varnothing$\pdfliteral}}
such that

n^{\prime},p,O\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt\bigl((\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v^{\prime})\ \mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}({\boldsymbol{\neg}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{propose}(v^{\prime}))\bigr),

from which it follows using Lemmas
4.2.1
and
2.2.4
and some basic reasoning on truth-values that

\text{either}\quad n^{\prime},O\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v^{\prime}\quad\text{or}\quad n^{\prime},O\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{\neg}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}.

Now
n<n^{\prime}
and we assumed in equation (
1
) that
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)
, so using

{\mathsf{(Pax2Twined)}}
and strong modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
2
))
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip-1.0pt\Diamond}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)
, and in particular (by routine reasoning on the clauses for
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip-1.0pt\Diamond}\hskip 0.0pt
in Figure
6
and for
\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt
in Figure
5
)

n,O\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)
.
Thus
n,O\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}
and by Lemma
4.5.2
(
1
) (taking
\phi={\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}
in that Lemma)

n^{\prime},O\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}
.
It follows that we are not in the right-hand (‘or’) branch above, so we must be in the left-hand (‘either’) branch, and thus
n^{\prime},O\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v^{\prime}
.

Using the
\mathsf{accept}
part of the inductive hypothesis,
v^{\prime}=v
.
∎

Theorem 7.3.6

(Agreement)
.

We have:

\text{TyPax}\vDash{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v,v^{\prime}.({\hbox{\hskip 3.22917pt\hskip-3.22917pt\hbox{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hskip-3.22917pt\hskip-0.25pt\raisebox{-0.6pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.30554pt}{0.77496pt}\hskip 0.5pt}}\hskip-0.25pt\hskip 3.22917pt}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v)\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v^{\prime})\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}v^{\prime}{\boldsymbol{\text{=}}}v).

Proof.

Suppose
v,v^{\prime}\in\mathord{\text{Value}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle\geq 0}
and
\varsigma
is a valuation such that
\vDash_{\varsigma}\text{TyPax}
.
We use strong modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
2
)), so suppose

n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\hbox{\hskip 3.22917pt\hskip-3.22917pt\hbox{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hskip-3.22917pt\hskip-0.25pt\raisebox{-0.6pt}{\hbox{\rule{4.30554pt}{0.77496pt}\hskip 0.5pt}}\hskip-0.25pt\hskip 3.22917pt}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v)\quad\text{and}\quad n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v^{\prime}).

If
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v)
then we use Proposition
7.3.3
.

So suppose
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{decide}(v)
.
By
{\mathsf{(PaxDecideL?)}}
(and
{\mathsf{(PaxDecide{\boldsymbol{\neg}}L?)}}
if needed),
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)
and
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v^{\prime})
, so that by Proposition
4.4.3
(
4
) also

.
By Proposition
7.3.5
(
1
)
v^{\prime}=v
as required.
∎

7.4
Termination

Remark 7.4.1
.

Termination
is expressed in English as:

Every correct participant eventually decides some value.

We render this in our logic in Theorem
7.4.7
as

\text{TyPax}\vDash\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{infinitely}$}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.\mathsf{decide}(v).

In words:

There is always some future time when all participants decide.

Note that there is no need to explicitly specify ‘all correct/uncrashed participants’ above.
This is taken care of by our logic’s in-built notion of validity, since incorrect participants return
{\mathbf{b}}
, which is valid as per Remark
2.2.2
(
3
) (but not true or correct; cf. Remark
2.2.2
).

7.4.1
Global stabilisation of logical time (GSLT)

Our forward rules are wrapped in an

\mathsf{ultimately}

modality, to reflect an assumption that while there may be some initial network instability, if we wait long enough the network will stabilise (see the discussion in Remark
6.5.3
).
This leads us to the notion of
global stabilisation of logical time
, or
GSLT
for short:

Definition 7.4.2

(GSLT)
.

Suppose
\phi
is a closed predicate and
\varsigma
is a valuation.
Then:

1.

Say that
\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi
(i.e.
\phi
under an

\mathsf{ultimately}

modality) is
synchronous at
n
in
\varsigma
when
n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{forever}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi
.
53
53

53

By Definition
3.6.1
,
n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{forever}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi
means that
n^{\prime},p^{\prime},O^{\prime}\vDash_{\varsigma}\phi
for every
n^{\prime}>n
and every
O^{\prime}\in\mathord{\text{Open}}
and every
p^{\prime}\in O^{\prime}
.

Subtlety alert:
This is not quite the same thing as just
n^{\prime}\vDash_{\varsigma}\phi
for every
n^{\prime}>n
. The reader might like to think about why.
(Hint:
p\in O
.)

That is:

\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi\ \ \text{ is synchronous at $n$ in $\varsigma$}\quad\text{when}\quad n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{forever}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi.

In words,
\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi
is synchronous when the ‘

\mathsf{ultimately}

’ becomes ‘at every
O
, at every
p\in O
, at every future time’.

2.

Suppose the valuation
\varsigma
is such that
\vDash_{\varsigma}\text{TyPax}
(
\vDash_{\varsigma}
is from Figure
7
and
TyPax
is from Figure
11
).

Then define
\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)\in\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle\geq 0}
to be the least stage
n
such that all of the rules in Figure
11
that start with

\mathsf{ultimately}

— namely, the forward rules
{\mathsf{(PaxSend!)}}
,
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite!)}}
,
{\mathsf{(PaxAccept!)}}
,
{\mathsf{(PaxDecideL!)}}
, and
{\mathsf{(PaxDecide{\boldsymbol{\neg}}L!)}}
— are synchronous at
n
in the valuation
\varsigma
.

We call
\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
the
Global Stabilisation of Logical Time
in
\varsigma
.

Remark 7.4.3
.

We make some observations about Definition
7.4.2
.
Suppose
\phi
is a closed predicate.
Then:

1.

Unpacking Definition
7.4.2
(
1
) using the clause for

\mathsf{forever}

in Figure
6
,

\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi\text{ is synchronous at $n$ in $\varsigma$}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad n\vDash_{\varsigma}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{forever}$}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}\phi

and
\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
is least such that
\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)\vDash_{\varsigma}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{forever}$}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}\mathrel{\to}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send})
, and
\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)\vDash_{\varsigma}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{forever}$}\hskip 0.0pt\bigl((\mathsf{leader}\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send})\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose})\mathrel{\to}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{write}\bigr)
, and so on.

2.

If
\phi
is absolutely pointwise — meaning by Definition
4.7.2
that
{[\hskip-1.99997pt[\phi]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{n,p,O,\varsigma}={[\hskip-1.99997pt[\phi]\hskip-1.99997pt]}_{n,p,O^{\prime},\varsigma}
always, so that the truth-value of
\phi
does not depend on the open set argument
O
— then we can simplify further:

\begin{array}[]{r@{\ }l}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi\text{ is synchronous at $n$ in $\varsigma$}\quad\Longleftrightarrow&n\vDash_{\varsigma}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{forever}$}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}\phi\\
\quad\Longleftrightarrow&\forall n^{\prime}{>}n.(n^{\prime}\vDash_{\varsigma}\phi).\end{array}

The reader can easily check that the forward rules
{\mathsf{(PaxSend!)}}
,
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite!)}}
,
{\mathsf{(PaxAccept!)}}
,
{\mathsf{(PaxDecideL!)}}
, and
{\mathsf{(PaxDecide{\boldsymbol{\neg}}L!)}}
from
TyPax
in Figure
11
are all of the form
\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi
for absolutely pointwise
\phi
.

3.

\mathit{GSLT}
is well-defined in Definition
7.4.2
(
2
) because there are finitely many

\mathsf{ultimately}

-axioms in
TyPax
, mentioning finitely many

\mathsf{ultimately}

modalities, and a finite set of natural numbers has a finite least upper bound
n^{\prime}
.
It is clear by construction that if
\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt\phi
is synchronous at
n
then it is synchronous at every
n^{\prime\prime}>n
, so in particular all the

\mathsf{ultimately}

-axioms are synchronous at
n^{\prime\prime}=n^{\prime}
.
54
54

54

It is important that
\phi
be closed for this argument to work.
For instance, if we had written
{\mathsf{(PaxAccept!)}}
as
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}(v)\mathrel{\to}\mathsf{accept}(v))
— which is a distinct assertion from that used in Figure
11
— then
\mathit{GSLT}
might not be defined.
Also the fine design of the

\mathsf{ultimately}

modality, matters.
For instance, if we had defined

\mathsf{ultimately}

to be
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{sometime}$}\hskip 0.0pt\,\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{forever}$}\hskip 0.0pt
rather than
\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{sometime}$}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{forever}$}\hskip 0.0pt
, then the arguments might fail (consider the case of
\mathord{\text{Point}}
infinite).

Lemma 7.4.4
.

Suppose
\varsigma
is a valuation such that
\vDash_{\varsigma}\text{TyPax}
.
Suppose
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle\geq 0}
and
n>\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
and
v\in\mathord{\text{Value}}
.
Then

n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}(v)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v).

Proof.

We prove a cycle of implications, freely using weak and strong modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
1
&
2
)).

•

Suppose
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}(v)
.

By
{\mathsf{(PaxAccept!)}}
(since
n>\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
),

\forall p\in\mathord{\text{Point}},O\in\mathord{\text{Open}}_{\hskip-0.5pt\scalebox{0.6}{\pdfliteral${\neq}\varnothing$\pdfliteral}}.n,p,O\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{accept}
.
Restricting to
p\in O
in the quantification, we have that
n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{accept}
.
By Proposition
4.8.9
(
2
) and
{\mathsf{(PaxPCorrect)}}
(for
\mathsf{accept}
),

n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{accept}
.
Unpacking the existential
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{accept}
and using
{\mathsf{(PaxAccept?)}}
and Lemma
7.3.2
(
1
), we conclude that
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)
.

•

Suppose
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}(v)
.

By Proposition
4.4.3
(
4
)
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v)
.

•

Suppose
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{accept}(v)
.

By
{\mathsf{(PaxAccept?)}}

n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}(v)
.
∎

Lemma 7.4.5
.

Suppose
\varsigma
is a valuation such that
\vDash_{\varsigma}\text{TyPax}
.
Suppose
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle\geq 0}
and
n>\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
.
Then:

1.

n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}\mathrel{\to}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send}
.

2.

n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send}
.

Proof.

We consider each part in turn:

1.

We use weak modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
1
)).
Suppose
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}
.
By
{\mathsf{(PaxSend!)}}
(since
n>\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
)

\forall p\in\mathord{\text{Point}}.n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send}.

By Lemma
4.7.7
(
2
)
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send}
is pointwise, so by Proposition
4.7.14
(
2
)
n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send}
as required.

2.

We use strong modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
2
)).
Suppose
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}
.
By part
1
of this result and weak modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
1
)),
n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send}
.
By
{\mathsf{(PaxPCorrect)}}
(for
\mathsf{send}
)
n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{send}]
, so by
Proposition
4.8.9
(
2
)

n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send}
as required.
∎

7.4.2
GSLT implies termination

Recall from Definition
4.8.2
(
2
) that
\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{decide}]={\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.\mathsf{T}\hskip-2.5pt\mathsf{F}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{decide}(v)
.

Lemma 7.4.6
.

Suppose
\varsigma
is a valuation such that
\vDash_{\varsigma}\text{TyPax}
.
Suppose
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle\geq 0}
and
n>\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
, and
p\in\mathord{\text{Point}}
.
Then:

1.

If
n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}
and
n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{write}]
then
n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt(\mathsf{leader}\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{write})
.

2.

\vDash_{\varsigma}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{infinitely}$}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}(\mathsf{leader}\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{decide}]\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{write})
.

3.

\vDash_{\varsigma}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{infinitely}$}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}(\mathsf{leader}\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{decide}])\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept})
.

Proof.

We consider each part in turn:

1.

Suppose
n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}
.
By
{\mathsf{(PaxPropose?)}}
and strong modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
2
))
n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{leader}
.
By Lemma
7.4.5
(
2
) and strong modus ponens
n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send}
.
Using weak modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
1
)) and
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite!)}}
(since
n>\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
)
n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{write}
.
By Proposition
4.8.8
(
3
) (since
n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{write}]
)
n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{write}
.

2.

By
{\mathsf{(LdrExist)}}
, and
{\mathsf{(LdrCorrect)}}
with Lemma
4.5.3
(for
\mbox{$\mathit{tvs}$}=\{{\mathbf{t}}\}
in that Lemma), and strong modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
2
)),
for infinitely many
n>\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
there exists
l_{n}\in\mathord{\text{Point}}
such that

n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{leader}\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{propose},\mathsf{write},\mathsf{decide}].

(We can equivalently write
\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{leader}
or just
\mathsf{leader}
above, because
{\mathsf{(LdrExist)}}
insists that
\mathsf{leader}
is two-valued.)
So for each
n
and
l_{n}
we have
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{decide}]
.
We will now check that
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{write}
.
We reason as follows:

•

By
{\mathsf{(PaxPropose!)}}
(since
n>\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
) and weak modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
1
)) we have that
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}
.

•

By Proposition
4.8.8
(
3
) (since
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{propose}]
and
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}
)
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{propose}
.

•

By part
1
of this result (since
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{write}]
)
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{write}
.

3.

We just combine part
2
of this result with Lemma
7.4.4
.
∎

Recall that Theorem
7.4.7
expresses termination (as the name suggests) but also, taken together with Agreement, it expresses an abstracted form of the Integrity correctness property by virtue of the use of the

\mathsf{infinitely}

modality, as per Remark
7.1.2
:

Theorem 7.4.7

(Termination)
.

We have:

\text{TyPax}\vDash\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{infinitely}$}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}.

Proof.

Suppose
\varsigma
is a valuation and
\vDash_{\varsigma}\text{TyPax}
.
By Lemmas
7.4.6
(
3
) and
4.5.3
(for
\mbox{$\mathit{tvs}$}=\{{\mathbf{t}}\}
in that Lemma), for infinitely many
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle\geq 0}
there exists an
l_{n}\in\mathord{\text{Point}}
such that

1.

n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{leader}
,

2.

n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{decide}]
(Definition
4.8.2
(
2
)), and

3.

n\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}
.

By
{\mathsf{(PaxDecideL!)}}
(since
n>\mbox{$\mathit{GSLT}$}(\varsigma)
)
and weak modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
1
))
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}
.
By Proposition
4.8.8
(
3
) (since
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{decide}]
and
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}
)
n,l_{n}\vDash_{\varsigma}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}
.

It follows using
{\mathsf{(PaxDecide{\boldsymbol{\neg}}L!)}}
and weak modus ponens (Proposition
2.3.3
(
1
)) that
n,p\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}
for non-leader
p\in\mathord{\text{Point}}
and thus for
every

p\in\mathord{\text{Point}}
, and so by (Lemma
4.7.7
(
2
) and) Proposition
4.7.14
(
2
) that
n\vDash_{\varsigma}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}
.

The above holds for infinitely many
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\scriptscriptstyle\geq 0}
, so by Lemma
4.5.3
(for
\mbox{$\mathit{tvs}$}=\{{\mathbf{t}},{\mathbf{b}}\}
in that Lemma) we have that
\vDash_{\varsigma}\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{infinitely}$}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}
as required.
∎

Remark 7.4.8
.

Theorem
7.4.7
proves that
\text{TyPax}\vDash\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{infinitely}$}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}
holds.
Note that
{\mathbf{b}}
(the truth-value associated to a crashed process) is a valid truth-value, so
n\vDash{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.3pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Box\hskip 0.0pt}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}
can hold in the trivial case that everyone is crashed.
Thus, what Theorem
7.4.7
asserts in English is that infinitely often, everyone who is not crashed, decides (but it might be that everyone is crashed) — from which it obviously follows that if a participant never crashes then eventually it decides a value, as per Remark
7.1.1
.

But in fact, we can prove a bit more:

\text{TyPax}\vDash\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{infinitely}$}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide}

In words: infinitely often, there exists a
quorum of uncrashed participants
, who decide.

This follows from Theorem
7.4.7
by easy reasoning using
{\mathsf{(PaxPCorrect)}}
,
{\mathsf{(PaxDecide{\boldsymbol{\neg}}L!)}}
, and Proposition
7.3.3
; we leave this to the reader to check.

8
Simpler Declarative Paxos

Logic is useful for simplification and abstraction in many ways.
In particular, we can axiomatise something, prove some properties — and then look at what we actually needed from the axioms to make the proofs work.
This can feed back to suggest refinements and generalisations of the original axioms.
(For example: many properties of natural numbers are actually properties of fields, or rings, or integral domains, and so on.)

We can do something similar here, and look for the most elementary set of axioms that are actually required for our correctness proofs as written:

8.1
What we used in the correctness proofs for
TyPax

Remark 8.1.1
.

We note a curious thing about our correctness proofs in Section
7
, that we never used axioms
{\mathsf{(PaxSend?)}}
or
{\mathsf{(PaxPropose01)}}
.

How can this be?
Is this an error?

No.
This is an opportunity to further simplify the Declarative Paxos from Figure
11
to the
Simpler Declarative Paxos
in Figure
12
.
To understand how and why this works, we need to highlight some details about how the Paxos algorithm works.

Remark 8.1.2

(Why we did not use
{\mathsf{(PaxSend?)}}
)
.

Recall from Remark
6.2.1
our high-level view of the Paxos algorithm, and recall that
TyPax
in Figure
11
is a declarative version of that algorithm (obtained by abstracting away explicit message-passing and algorithmic time, as per Remark
6.2.2
).

Note of the algorithm that

•

in step
1
of Remark
6.2.1
, the leader broadcasts a ‘propose’ message; and

•

in step
2
, a participant
p
that receives that ‘propose’ message, replies with a ‘send’ message carrying as its payload a value
v_{p}
, which is
p
’s most recently accepted value (if any); and

•

in step
3
, the leader gathers the
v_{p}
values from these responses and, if it has a quorum (nonempty open set) of responses, it selects a most recent value to use in its write step.

In the algorithm, the only way that the leader
l
can know of a participant
p
’s value of
v_{p}
, is if
l
receives a send message from
p
with payload
v_{p}
.
If
l
receives no such message, then it does not know the value of
v_{p}
.

In contrast, predicates of
{\mathbf{3}}\hskip-0.5pt\upmu\mathsf{QL}
are not assigned truth-values by participants in an algorithm; they are assigned truth-values as if by an omniscient observer who is standing outside of the model and who has full (omniscient) access to it.
This is reflected in
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite?)}}
, where we write
(\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept})\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v
.
This directly examines the model to find the most recently accepted value.
No reference here is required to the payload of the quorum of
\mathsf{send}
messages.

{\mathsf{(PaxWrite?)}}
still insists that such a quorum exists, as per the
\Box\hskip 0.0pt{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{send}
that appears in
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite?)}}
, so in this case the value
(\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept})\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v
is computable by the leader, and we could specify a more complex predicate that checks the set of
v_{p}
payloads in the send messages of that quorum to select a most recently accepted value.
In the implementation, this is necessary because the leader
l
is not omniscient.
But in the logic we have no reason to go to this trouble, because we can just read the information directly from the model using
(\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept})\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v
.
55
55

55

For example: if we want to compute
100!
in the real world, we have to build a computer to do this for us, which involves many practical details including the construction of a semiconductor industry, compiler design and so on. In logic, however, we can just say that it is
\prod_{i=1}^{100}i
, because that is what
100!

is
.

So our axioms are just doing their job, which is to be a declarative abstraction of the implementation.
But since we do not use
{\mathsf{(PaxSend?)}}
, this is evidence that we may treat the
\mathsf{send}
step as an implementational detail having to do with how information moves around the network, and elide it.

Remark 8.1.3

(Why we did not use
{\mathsf{(PaxPropose01)}}
)
.

This is due to another particularity of how the Paxos algorithm works.
The leader chooses one value in the propose step (or no value, if the leader has crashed), but it only uses that value in the case that it finds a quorum of participants who have never previously accepted any value (this is the right-hand disjunct in
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite?)}}
).
Just in this case, it writes the value that it chose in the propose step.

So what really matters is that the leader
only writes one value
.
If the leader had proposed two values, this would be fine — provided that it only writes one of them.

Leaders in the Paxos algorithm do not bother with multiple values in the propose step, because this would be a waste of time; they know they will only ever need at most one.
Thus,
{\mathsf{(PaxPropose01)}}
is right to reflect a true fact of the algorithm that it does not propose any more than the value that it will need, but this is an algorithmic efficiency not a logical necessity.
Likewise, the logic is right to point this out to us by
not
requiring
{\mathsf{(PaxPropose01)}}
in the proofs: so long as at most one value gets
written
— which is handled by
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite01)}}
— none of our correctness properties are affected.

Remark 8.1.4
.

By examining the proofs further, some further possible simplifications are revealed:

1.

We can combine rules
{\mathsf{(PaxWrite01)}}
and
{\mathsf{(LdrUniq)}}
, as per the discussion in Subsection
7.3.2
, to obtain an elegantly concise rule
{\mathsf{(SPaxWrite01)}}
.

2.

We used
{\mathsf{(PaxPCorrect)}}
in our proofs to obtain
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{P}]
only twice:

•

in Lemma
7.4.4
for
\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{accept}
, and

•

in Lemma
7.4.5
for
\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{send}
.

We have elided the
send
step in
\Theta_{\text{TySPax}}
, so we only need to consider
\mathsf{accept}
.
Accordingly we take
{\mathsf{(SPaxPCorrect)}}
to be just
{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{correct}[\mathsf{accept}]
.

Remark 8.1.5
.

In conclusion, we see that the proofs in Section
7
are not only correctness proofs, but can also be read as an active commentary on the axioms.
Our logic has done what logic does, and has separated what is logically necessary from what is implementationally required or implementationally convenient.
This shows us how to simplify the axiomatisation (if we wish) further away from the implementation, and closer towards what is logically essential:

8.2
The definition

\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad}r@{\ }l}\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\lx@text@underline{\emph{Backward rules, for backward inference}}\hss}}\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathsf{(SPaxPropose?)}}&&{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.\ \mathsf{propose}(v)\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}\mathsf{leader}\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathsf{(SPaxWrite?)}}&&{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.\ \mathsf{write}(v)\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}\begin{array}[t]{l}\mathsf{leader}\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}\hskip 0.0pt(\\
\quad\bigl(((\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept})\mathbin{\hbox to0.0pt{\scalebox{0.73}{${\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}$}\hss}\hskip-2.0pt\rightarrow}v)\mathbin{\boldsymbol{\vee}}(\mathsf{propose}(v)\mathrel{\boldsymbol{\wedge}}{\boldsymbol{\neg}}\mathsf{T}\hskip 0.0pt{\reflectbox{$\mathsf{R}$}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept})\bigr))\end{array}\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathsf{(SPaxAccept?)}}&&{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\forall$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}v.\ \mathsf{accept}(v)\mathrel{\rightharpoonup}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}(v)\\[4.30554pt]

\\[4.30554pt]
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{\lx@text@underline{\emph{Forward rules, for making progress}}\hss}}\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathsf{(SPaxWrite!)}}&&\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt(\mathsf{leader}\mathrel{\to}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{write})\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathsf{(SPaxAccept!)}}&&\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\hskip 0.0pt\Diamond\hskip 0.0pt}\mathsf{write}\mathrel{\to}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{accept})\\[4.30554pt]
{\mathsf{(SPaxDecide!)}}&&\scalebox{0.8}[1.0]{$\mathsf{ultimately}$}\hskip 0.0pt({\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}^{\hskip-0.6pt\mathord{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\displaystyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\textstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}{\scalebox{0.65}{\pdfliteral$\scriptscriptstyle\mathgeo{Q}$\pdfliteral}}}}_{\Box}\hskip 0.0pt\mathsf{accept}\mathrel{\to}{\boldsymbol{{\hskip 0.8pt\raisebox{0.5pt}{\scalebox{.85}{$\exists$}}\hskip 0.2pt}}}\mathsf{decide})\\[4.30554pt]
